Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I really hope I'm not taking up too much of your time. 

Um... well, I came in mostly because my husband and some of the folks at the warehouse where I work, they keep acting like I'm... well, they say I'm acting crazy. I hate using that word, and I'm really not trying to cause any trouble for anybody, I promise. It's just... I've been noticing things. I know they've been watching my moves on the floor. And they keep changing my pallet counts when I'm not looking. 

I'm pretty sure they're just doing it to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. I just want to do a good job and move up, you know? Is it okay that I'm upset about that? I mean, anybody would be frustrated by that, right?

Is it normal to get a little short-tempered like that when you're just trying to stay focused? I hope I'm explaining this right.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is around?\n\nPatient: Um... voices? Oh, gosh. I know how that sounds. Please don't think I'm losing my mind or anything, I really don't want you to think I'm crazy. It's not like I'm hearing anything bad or scary, I promise! \n\nIt's just... well, sometimes when I'm pulling pallets in the aisle at work, or even just at home folding clothes, I'll hear a man's voice. It sounds clear as day, like he's standing right next to me, and he just says, \"Nicole, check it again.\" But then I look around and nobody is anywhere near me. \n\nI mean, I always do check my work again when I hear it, because I really want to make sure everything is perfect. I figure that's probably just my brain's way of making sure I'm doing a good job, right? It's normal to get a little... over-focused like that when you're stressed and trying your best, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: When you hear that man's voice, is it only during those times when you feel especially fired up and full of energy?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, actually. I hope that's okay to say. I mean, I know it would probably make more sense if it only happened when I was all fired up, but... well, there was a stretch recently, maybe a few weeks, where I was actually pretty quiet. I was sleeping almost normal hours again, and I wasn't rushing around trying to do a million things at once. I was just trying to be calm and steady, you know?\n\nBut even then... I still heard him. Just that same voice, telling me to check things again. And I still knew they were messing with my pallet counts at work, even when I was feeling totally level. I really hope that doesn't sound too strange... it just feels like I have to stay on top of things no matter how my energy is, right? That makes sense, doesn't it?\n\nDoctor: About how long have the voice and the feeling that people are tampering with your pallet counts been going on?\n\nPatient: Um, how long? Oh, gosh. I... I guess the voice, and that feeling that people were watching me or changing my things around... it's been happening for about eight months now. \n\nEight months sounds like a really long time when I say it out loud, doesn't it? That's understandable, right? I hope I didn't mess up by waiting so long to tell someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any major medical or neurologic illness, such as seizures, a head injury, or a brain condition?\n\nPatient: Um... no, nothing like that. But no, I've never had any seizures or brain things... and I've never hit my head bad enough to need a doctor or anything. \n\nI mean, I'm pretty healthy overall! I hope that helps rule out anything too scary.\n\nDoctor: Over the past several months, have you found yourself feeling much less interested in people or activities you used to care about?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I get home from the warehouse and I'm so tired, you know? \n\nSo mostly I just sit quiet on the couch and stare at the TV or scroll on my phone. I don't really feel like doing much else. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... gosh, well... no, I don't think so. I mean, I hope I'm not forgetting something important, but I don't remember anyone ever saying I was talking too much. 

I try really hard to be polite and listen to people, especially at work. I don't want to be one of those people who just talks over everyone else, you know? So even when I have all that energy, I try to keep to myself and just focus on moving the pallets. 

Maybe I talk a little faster sometimes just because I'm moving faster, but no one's ever told me it was too much or asked me to be quiet. I hope that's okay to say. I just really try to be respectful of everyone else's space. Does that make sense?

Is it okay that I don't really get depressed like that?\n\n[Last Question]\nOver the past ten months, have the times when you felt unusually energized or irritable made up most of that whole period?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / laborer_or_freight_stock_or_material_mover / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Additional Requirement 2\nmanifestation: \"For more than half of this whole stretch, I have had those fired-up periods where I sleep very little, take on too much, and get irritable when anyone tries to slow me down.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... gosh. I want to be completely honest with you, even though I feel a little bad admitting it. But... yes, I guess so. If I look at that whole stretch, probably for more than half of it, I've had those really... fired-up periods. 

I just sleep very little during those times, and I end up taking on way too much at the warehouse because I really just want to be as helpful as possible, you know? I always want to pull my weight. But... the part I'm kind of ashamed of is... I can get a bit irritable when anyone tries to slow me down. 

I really don't mean to be rude to my coworkers, I promise! I just get so focused on getting everything done and moving the freight. I hope you don't think I'm a mean person for getting snappy like that. I really try my best to be good to everyone. Does that make sense?

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... serious problems? Oh gosh... I really hate to even talk about this part. I always want to be the best worker I can be, but... well, I guess there were some hiccups. I hope you don't judge me too harshly for this, but my supervisors... they actually had to take me off the forklift and some of the other safety-sensitive jobs. 

It wasn't because I was doing anything bad on purpose, I promise! It's just... with all that energy, my counts and my regular routines got a little jumbled, and I guess I was actually slowing the line down instead of helping. I felt so terrible about it because I never want to be the one holding up the freight. 

And, um... at home... this is the part I'm really ashamed of. My husband... he told me he just can't rely on me the way he used to when I get all fired up like that. I'm really trying to fix it, though. I want to be a good wife and a good employee, I really do. I'm doing the right thing by being honest with you about all this, aren't I? I just hope you don't think I'm a bad person for letting things slip.

I would never do that. But... energized? I mean, I guess I have been having some days where I just feel really... fired up. Like I've got all this energy and nobody can slow me down. I get a lot done on those days, which is a good thing, right? I just want to be a hard worker. \n\nBut, um... I do feel kind of awful about this one part. I haven't really told anyone because I don't want you to think I'm a bad wife or anything... but lately, if my husband asks me even one small question, like about the kids or what my shift schedule is for the week, I just... I snap at him. I really don't mean to! It just suddenly feels like he's trying to control me, you know? I'm trying to be better about it, I really am. Is it normal to get a little short-tempered like that when you're just trying to stay focused? I hope I'm explaining this right.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is around?\n\nPatient: Um... voices? Oh, gosh. I know how that sounds. Please don't think I'm losing my mind or anything, I really don't want you to think I'm crazy. It's not like I'm hearing anything bad or scary, I promise! \n\nIt's just... well, sometimes when I'm pulling pallets in the aisle at work, or even just at home folding clothes, I'll hear a man's voice. It sounds clear as day, like he's standing right next to me, and he just says, \"Nicole, check it again.\" But then I look around and nobody is anywhere near me. \n\nI mean, I always do check my work again when I hear it, because I really want to make sure everything is perfect. I figure that's probably just my brain's way of making sure I'm doing a good job, right? It's normal to get a little... over-focused like that when you're stressed and trying your best, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: When you hear that man's voice, is it only during those times when you feel especially fired up and full of energy?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, actually. I hope that's okay to say. I mean, I know it would probably make more sense if it only happened when I was all fired up, but... well, there was a stretch recently, maybe a few weeks, where I was actually pretty quiet. I was sleeping almost normal hours again, and I wasn't rushing around trying to do a million things at once. I was just trying to be calm and steady, you know?\n\nBut even then... I still heard him. Just that same voice, telling me to check things again. And I still knew they were messing with my pallet counts at work, even when I was feeling totally level. I really hope that doesn't sound too strange... it just feels like I have to stay on top of things no matter how my energy is, right? I guess the voice, and that feeling that people were watching me or changing my things around... it's been happening for about eight months now. \n\nEight months sounds like a really long time when I say it out loud, doesn't it? That's understandable, right? I hope I didn't mess up by waiting so long to tell someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any major medical or neurologic illness, such as seizures, a head injury, or a brain condition?\n\nPatient: Um... no, nothing like that. I just think I'm drained from trying to keep everything straight at work. That's pretty normal when you're working hard, isn't it?
